# CatalogueService

> Generated by `tools/build-service-docs.py`. **Do not edit.**

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Tier | commerce: The sale path. Highest availability, highest write rate. |
| Contracts | `catalogue`, `promotions`, `seating` |
| Schemas owned | `catalogue`, `pricing`, `promotions`, `seating` |
| Operations in the slice | 89 of 475 |
| Scale | Read-heavy, bundle-published to tills. The catalogue bundle is this service's output (ADR-0013). |
| If it is down | A bad publish reaches every workstation. Versioned and rollback-able for that reason. |

## Why this is a service

**What is sold, at what price, in which seat.** Promotions and seating are folded in because both are pricing and availability against the same product — a promotion that cannot see a price list and a seat that cannot see a product are two round trips on the hot path of every sale. **`pricing` joined it on 20 September**, which closes decision 5 of the schema merge — it took all three `pricing` tables and said the schema needed an owner, and that stayed open because the tables had no operations and a service is inferred from the contract that declares them. The operations were written into `catalogue.yaml`, beside the price lists and products a dynamic rule adjusts: a rule that reads `catalogue.price_list` and `catalogue.product` to compute a price belongs on the side of the boundary those live on, or every evaluation is a cross-service call on the sale path.

## Group: availability

### getSeatAvailability

**`GET /performances/{performanceId}/seat-availability`**: Seat status for a performance

Every seat with its current state — available, held, sold, blocked or buffered.
**Not offline-capable.** Seated inventory cannot be leased, so a terminal without a connection cannot sell seats.
**A guest calls this without a permission and sees published data only** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Pattern 4; CHG-GCF-001; `x-ticvai-guest-callable`): a guest or a visitor gets the seat availability of a published performance, and nothing in draft, unpublished or withdrawn; asked for by id, such a record answers as not found. `PRODUCT_VIEW` is what a staff caller must hold, and staff also see drafts (ADR-0025).

| Permission | `PRODUCT_VIEW` |

| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |

| Conflict policy | serverWins |

| Guest callable | True |
| Reads | `seating.seat`, `seating.seat_category`, `seating.seat_reservation` |
| Writes | - |
| Called by | BO-015, BO-019, BO-1000, BO-1001, BO-1002, BO-1039, BO-1040, BO-986, BO-994, BO-995, BO-996, GST-049, POS-004, WEB-007 |

## Group: bundle

### createBundle

**`POST /bundles`**: Create a bundle

A bundle is a product whose price differs from the sum of its parts. **The allocation split is mandatory** — without it the ledger cannot divide the revenue, and each component may sit in a different venue, account, tax treatment or legal entity.
**Allocation rule (decided 28 September, audit R101):** the bundle price is allocated to components in proportion to their list price, and **list price is the component variant's current price** when the bundle is created. Rounding is to the currency's minor unit, and **the rounding remainder goes to the first component**, so the allocated amounts always sum to the bundle price exactly. `proRataListPrice` is the default method; `percentage` and `fixedAmount` remain for an explicit split and follow the same remainder rule.
**Writes `promotions.promotion_audit`** in the same transaction, as every promotions configuration change does (entityType `bundle`, eventType `created`). Capacity policies and partner product mappings are set afterwards by setBundleCapacityPolicy and setBundlePartnerProductMappings (decided 29 September, writers pass).
**A meal combo with admission** (decided 29 September, MOB-4) is a bundle with an `admission` component and a `fnbMenuItem` component naming the menu item; a `fnbMenuItem` component with no `menuItemId`, or one whose menu item does not sell its `variantId`, is refused `422`.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | setup, makes `promotions.bundle`, `promotions.bundle_choice_group`, `promotions.bundle_choice_option`, `promotions.bundle_component` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `fnb.menu_item`, `promotions.allocation_component`, `promotions.bundle`, `promotions.bundle_choice_group`, `promotions.bundle_choice_option`, `promotions.bundle_component` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `promotions.allocation_component`, `promotions.bundle`, `promotions.bundle_choice_group`, `promotions.bundle_choice_option`, `promotions.bundle_component`, `promotions.promotion_audit` |
| Called by | BO-011 |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**: `CreateBundleRequest`

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| code | string | yes | (max length 64) |
| name | string | yes | (max length 200) |
| description | string |  | (max length 1000) |
| venueId | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| kind | BundleKind: enum (fixed, dynamic, mandatory, optional, promotional) | yes |  |
| price | Money | yes | On the wire this is three fields; in the database it is one column. |
| price.amount | string | yes | Decimal string, never a float. (pattern ^-?\d+(\.\d{1,4})?$) |
| price.currency | string | yes | Resolved from the region, not stored on the row (ADR-0018). (pattern ^[A-Z]{3}$) |
| price.scale | integer | yes | Resolved from the region alongside currency. (min 0; max 4) |
| components | array of BundleComponent | yes | (min items 0) |
| components[].id | string (uuid) |  | Added 20 August. (read-only) |
| components[].variantId | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| components[].componentKind | BundleComponentKind: enum (admission, fnbMenuItem, retail, addOn, other) |  | What a bundle component entitles the guest to (decided 29 September, MOB-4). (default admission) |
| components[].menuItemId | string (uuid) |  | The F&B menu item a fnbMenuItem component entitles the guest to (decided 29 September, MOB-4): the meal of a *meal combo with admission*. (nullable) |
| components[].redeemAtOutletIds | array of string (uuid) |  | Outlets that redeem an fnbMenuItem component; empty means any outlet of the component's venue that has the menu item on a live menu (MOB-4). (max items 20) |
| components[].quantity | integer | yes | (min 1) |
| components[].isOptional | boolean |  | (default False) |
| components[].substituteVariantIds | array of string (uuid) |  | For dynamic bundles — guest chooses among these. |
| components[].substitutionTriggers | array of enum (soldOut, capacityExhausted, productSuspended, venueClosed, externalApiUnavailable, inventoryBelowThreshold) |  | When a substitute from substituteVariantIds may replace this component (Dynamic Component Substitution Engine). (nullable) |
| components[].substitutionPriceEffect | enum (samePrice, surcharge, reducedPrice) |  | What a substitution does to the bundle price. (default samePrice) |
| components[].substitutionApproval | enum (none, customer, operator) |  | Who must accept a substitution before it stands. (default customer) |
| components[].venueId | string (uuid) |  | Where this component is redeemed. (nullable) |
| choiceGroups | array of BundleChoiceGroup |  | Dynamic bundles (3.5.10). |
| choiceGroups[].id | string (uuid) |  | (read-only) |
| choiceGroups[].label | string | yes | What the guest is asked. |
| choiceGroups[].choose | integer | yes | How many options the guest picks. (min 1) |
| choiceGroups[].allowDuplicates | boolean |  | Whether the same option may be picked twice. (default False) |
| choiceGroups[].options | array of object | yes | The rows of promotions.bundle_choice_option, one per option, each keyed to its group. (min items 2) |
| choiceGroups[].options[].id | string (uuid) |  | (read-only) |
| choiceGroups[].options[].variantId | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| choiceGroups[].options[].quantity | integer |  | (default 1) |

### getGuestBundle

**`GET /guest-bundles/{bundleId}`**: What a guest is shown of a bundle

A published bundle as a guest or a visitor sees it (R8 blocker ORDERS-MONEY-C32, the lead's answer A of 20 October 2026; GST-004, GST-056, WEB-008). Answers `GuestBundle`: name, description, kind, price and savings, components and choice groups, and validity, never the allocation, owner, campaign, code or whether it has been sold. A bundle in draft, unpublished or withdrawn answers 404. `getBundle` stays the staff read and is unchanged. Published catalogue data, so no permission is needed.

**Read under the venue the request names** (decided 25 October 2026, Chinmay, ADAM CR-198 option A; CHG-R10ID-010; the rule for every public guest read is on `guestAuth`): the guest app or site sends `X-Venue-Id`; the server checks it is one of the tenant's venues (else 404) and reads under a read-only scope of that venue's path, so `promotions.bundle`'s venue row-level security finds the venue's bundles with no session. An anonymous guest session carries the same venue scope (CR-183).
**What a guest sees** (ADAM CR-197, option B, decided 25 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-002). A bundle is **published** when `promotions.bundle.variant_id` is not null (r8, CHG-R8MIG-023: "null until published"), and **withdrawn** when `is_active` is false. A guest or a visitor gets the bundle only when `variant_id` is set, `is_active` is true and now is within `valid_from` / `valid_to` (a null bound is open); any other bundle, draft, unpublished, withdrawn or out of its window, answers 404, never a reason. `variant_id` is set by the step that creates the bundle's catalogue product, the publication step of `updateBundle`. `price` and `savingsAmount` are Money in the venue's currency and scale, read with `tenancy#venueSettingsRecords`.

**Enough to show and to add to the cart** (ADAM CR-597, option A with guest-only shapes, Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR597-001). `GuestBundle.variantId` is the bundle's own sellable variant (`promotions.bundle.variant_id`), required: every bundle a guest can see has one. `components` are `GuestBundleComponent` (id, variantId, name, quantity; description, componentKind, isOptional) and `choiceGroups` are `GuestBundleChoiceGroup` (id, label, choose, options; allowDuplicates) with `GuestBundleChoiceOption` options (id, variantId, name; description, quantity, isDefault). A name and description are the catalogue's public text for the variant: the product name, plus the variant name when the product has more than one variant. Every name is looked up in the same read, from `catalogue.product` and `catalogue.variant`, under the venue the request names, in one lookup for all the bundle's variant ids, not one per row. An option that is not offered (`unavailableBehaviour` `hideOption`) is left out; a group that can no longer be met answers 404, as any bundle a guest cannot buy. No menu item, redemption outlet or substitution field is sent, and no option price: the bundle price does not move with the choice. `BundleComponent`, `BundleChoiceGroup` and `getBundle` are unchanged, and nothing new is stored.

| Writes | - |
| Called by | GST-004, GST-056, WEB-008 |

### getLatestBundle

**`GET /catalogue/bundles/latest`**: Pull the current bundle for this workstation's venue

The primary terminal-facing endpoint. Returns the full bundle, or 304 when the supplied ETag matches.
Use `since` to request a delta instead of a full bundle — a terminal on a slow link should not re-download an unchanged catalogue.

### publishBundle

**`POST /catalogue/bundles`**: Compute, sign and publish a catalogue bundle

Snapshots current catalogue state for a venue, signs it, and publishes it for terminals to pull.
Publishing is the act that makes a configuration change visible at point of sale. Until a bundle is published, an edited price exists only in the back office.
**The bundle carries the venue's sale boards** (`platform.sale_board`, every page and tile; decided 28 September, audit R129 (4)). `tenancy.updateSaleBoard` promises its changes reach terminals with the next bundle, and this is where they do: the snapshot reads the venue's sale boards with the catalogue, so a board edit is live at the till once a bundle is published and applied. Tenancy owns the boards and both modules run in `commerce`, so the snapshot reads them in bulk through the views named for Catalogue (`platform.sale_board_for_catalogue`, `platform.sale_board_page_for_catalogue`, `platform.sale_board_tile_for_catalogue`; ADR-0055 rule 2, CHG-R6G-007), never the tables raw.
**A tile whose product cannot be sold is left out of the bundle** (decided 14 October 2026, Chinmay, ADAM CR-081; CHG-TEN-002). The board was checked against `variantsSellableAt` when it was saved, but a product can be withdrawn, archived or taken off sale after that. When the bundle is cut, a `product` tile whose product is not `isSellable` in the bundle being published is dropped from the bundle's copy of the board (the stored board keeps it), so the till never draws a button it cannot sell; the tile comes back with the first bundle in which its product is sellable again.

**Version, row and publisher** (ADAM CR-110, option 1 with option 2's stale default, decided 15 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-010). Each publish writes **one** `catalogue.published_bundle` row, the full snapshot (`isDelta` false); a terminal's delta is computed by `getLatestBundle` from its `since`. `version` is a per-venue integer, 1 for the venue's first bundle and one more than the venue's highest for each publish after, carried on the wire as its decimal string; `(venue_id, version)` is unique, so two publishes can never take one number. The row keeps `published_at` (the commit time), `published_by` (the caller) and the body's `note`, which `BundleSummary` returns. **staleAfterHours** defaults to the venue's offline policy `maxOfflineHours` (the policy saved at the venue's node, read with `tenancy#offlinePolicyRecords` by the venue's path from `tenancy#scopeRecords`; 24, the schema default, where none is saved); no second bound is configured. Signing is Ed25519 with the key `signatureKeyId` names (CHG-R8SPN-022). A `venueId` that names no venue the caller can see is 404.

| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.price`, `catalogue.price_list`, `catalogue.product`, `catalogue.published_bundle`, `catalogue.variant`, `platform.sale_board_for_catalogue`, `platform.sale_board_page_for_catalogue`, `platform.sale_board_tile_for_catalogue` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.published_bundle` |
| Called by | BO-013, BO-014, BO-037 |

### updateBundle

**`PATCH /bundles/{bundleId}`**: Amend a bundle

Components and allocation are immutable once the bundle has been sold. Historic orders reference the split that applied when they were placed, and the ledger is append-only.
**This body carries neither**, so it cannot change them: it amends the name, the price, the end date and whether the bundle is on sale. The one refusal it can meet is a price that breaks the split. Under a `fixedAmount` allocation the fixed amounts must sum to the bundle price (the same rule `createBundle` answers 400 for), and a new price with an unchanged split no longer does.
**Writes `promotions.promotion_audit`** in the same transaction, as every promotions configuration change does (entityType `bundle`, eventType `modified`, the changed fields before and after) (decided 29 September, writers pass).

**Publishing a bundle** (ADAM CR-197, option B, decided 25 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-002). `createBundle` stores a bundle unpublished: `variant_id` null and `is_active` false. The first `isActive: true` on a bundle whose `variant_id` is null is the **publication step**: in the same transaction it creates the bundle's catalogue product (kind `bundle`, its code and name the bundle's, one variant at the bundle's price; Catalogue owns `catalogue.product` and `catalogue.variant`, and Promotions runs in the same service) and writes that variant's id to `promotions.bundle.variant_id`. From then on the bundle is published: `isActive: false` withdraws it, and `isActive: true` puts it back on sale with the same product and variant. A guest sees it only while published, active and within its validity window (`getGuestBundle`).

**The split** (ADAM CR-110, option 1, decided 15 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-010). `promotions.allocation_split` and `promotions.allocation_component` are the record of the split; `promotions.bundle.allocation` is a copy derived from them in the same transaction and never read for a rule. The `fixedAmount` 400 is judged on `allocation_component.fixed_amount`. `savingsAmount` is the sum of the components' `list_price` captured on `allocation_component` when the bundle was created, less the bundle price; a later change to a variant's price does not move it. An unknown bundle, or one outside the caller's scope, is 404.

| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `promotions.allocation_component`, `promotions.bundle`, `promotions.bundle_choice_group`, `promotions.bundle_choice_option`, `promotions.bundle_component` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.product`, `catalogue.variant`, `promotions.allocation_component`, `promotions.allocation_split`, `promotions.bundle`, `promotions.bundle_component`, `promotions.promotion_audit` |
| Called by | BO-011 |

## Group: capacity

### getAvailability

**`GET /availability`**: Live remaining capacity

Display only. A terminal shows this to a guest but does not decide a sale on it — that is what leases are for. Under load this value is already stale by the time it renders.
**Every performance of an event in a window, in one call** (decided 29 September, rev 3 REV3-1). A guest date-and-time step draws up to 64 time tiles at once, paged `timesPerPage` at a time (`white-label.BookingFlowConfig.timesPerPage`) with day-part chips and their counts, and one request per performance is 64 round trips before the first tile. So `eventId` with `from` and `to` returns the remaining count of every performance of the event starting in that window, ordered by `startsAt`. `from` and `to` are required with `eventId` and the window is at most 31 days, else `400`. Exactly one of `performanceId`, `channelCapacityId` or `eventId` is given.
**The response is named** (`PerformanceAvailabilityPage` of `PerformanceAvailability`, rev 3 REV3-1), so screens bind to a schema rather than to an inline shape, and it is a `Page` like every list: a single `performanceId` is a one-page answer.
**A guest calls this without a permission and sees published data only** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Pattern 4; CHG-GCF-001; `x-ticvai-guest-callable`): a guest or a visitor gets the availability of published products, and nothing in draft, unpublished or withdrawn; asked for by id, such a record answers as not found. `PRODUCT_VIEW` is what a staff caller must hold, and staff also see drafts (ADR-0025).

| Read routing | primary |
| Guest callable | True |
| Reads | `catalogue.channel_capacity`, `catalogue.inventory_reservation`, `catalogue.performance` |
| Writes | - |
| Called by | BO-015, GST-004, GST-007, GST-050, KSK-004, KSK-005, KSK-015, POS-003, PTR-007, WEB-004, WEB-006 |

## Group: catalogue

### bulkChangePrices

**`POST /products/bulk-price`**: Reprice a category or a whole catalogue

2.9.9. **`BulkUpdateSeatsRequest` is the only bulk operation in the package** and it takes a selection plus the attribute to set — a usable precedent, applied here.
Repricing every adult admission before a season is routine and currently means one call per product. **Runs as a dry run first**, for the reason `assessProductChange` exists.
**Escalates to `PRICE_CONFIGURE` above a value threshold** (decided 28 September, audit R197): a run that changes any price by more than the venue setting `catalogue.bulkPriceChangeEscalationPercent` (proposed 10%) or touches more than `catalogue.bulkPriceChangeEscalationCount` prices (proposed 50) needs `PRICE_CONFIGURE`; below both, `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` is enough. Both are venue settings with a tenant default in `tenancy.VenueSettings`, proposed, client to correct. The dry run reports whether the run escalates; a real run that escalates without `PRICE_CONFIGURE` is a `403` (`price-escalation-required`).

**What a run touches and what it leaves** (ADAM CR-544; Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR544-001): it leaves untouched what its contract says it cannot touch, by rules on stored columns, and its result says whether the run escalates. **Selection:** `categoryId` (that exact category), `productKind` (the `ProductKind` enum), `priceListId` and `channel` combine with AND; with none given, every product of the venue is selected. `channel` is the catalogue `Channel` enum (pos, kiosk, web, mobile, b2b, ota, callCentre) and matches price lists whose `channels` contain it. A run changes `catalogue.price` (`Price.amount`) for the matched products' variants on the matched price lists, except these, which stay unchanged and are named in `skipped`: `partnerNetRate`, the price list's `price_list_type` is b2b, reseller or ota; `fixedPriceBundle`, the product has a `catalogue.package_pricing` row with `pricing_model` fixedPackagePrice, for that price list or with no price list; `openChangeRequest`, the product is named, by `change_request.product_id` or by a `change_request_line` with `object_type` product, in a pricing change request whose status is not rejected, published or rolledBack. `skipped` has one {productId, reason} per product and reason. `productsMatched` counts the products matched; `pricesChanged` counts the price rows changed, or that would change. **Adjustment:** `roundTo` rounds to the nearest multiple, half away from zero; without it, the result is rounded to the currency's scale. Any result below zero refuses the whole run with `400 validation` naming the price, and nothing changes. **Escalation:** `BulkPriceResult.escalates` is true when any changed price moves by more than `catalogue.bulkPriceChangeEscalationPercent`, or `pricesChanged` exceeds `catalogue.bulkPriceChangeEscalationCount` (venue settings). The dry run answers it. A real run that escalates, from a caller without `PRICE_CONFIGURE`, answers `403 price-escalation-required` and changes nothing. **effectiveFrom:** null, now or past applies now. A future `effectiveFrom` answers `400 validation` saying a future-dated change goes through a pricing change request, since `catalogue.price` stores no validity.

**What a run writes besides prices** (ADAM CR-580, Chinmay, 3 November 2026, superseding CR-558; CHG-CR580-001): a bulk price change is recorded in Catalogue's audit, one entry per changed price, and writes no version rows. A real run that changes prices writes, in the same transaction as the `catalogue.price` updates, one `catalogue.audit_entry` per changed price through the shared `AuditEntries.InsertAsync`, filled as the other pricing handlers fill it: `domain` pricing, `entity_type` price, `entity_id` the price id, `product_id` the price's product, `field` list_price, `previous_value` and `new_value` the old and new amount as decimal strings, the caller as actor, `occurred_at` the run's time. It writes no product version row: a product version is defined once by `updateProduct`, whose content hash and snapshot cover the product and its media, channel texts, sale permissions and owner forms, not prices, so a price-only change leaves that hash unchanged and there is no version. It writes no price-list version row: price-list versions are created only when an approved pricing change request publishes, and pricing rollback restores them from their snapshot; a price change that must be reversible by rollback goes through a pricing change request. A dry run, a skipped product, a price whose new amount equals the old one, and a refused run (400 or 403) write no audit entry. Not the kernel's audit trail (`IAuditTrail`): this operation's audit is `catalogue.audit_entry`.

### cloneProduct

**`POST /products/{productId}/clone`**: Copy a product as a new draft

1.1.47 and 1.1.48. `copyPriceList` and `cloneSeatMap` are the precedents and **the product equivalent did not exist**, so a seasonal variant was built by hand each year.
The clone starts as a draft with a new code. **Variants come with it; orders do not** — a clone has no history, which is the point of it being a clone rather than a version.

**What it writes, and what it refuses** (r13 root R21, CHG-R13R21-001). The new `catalogue.product` row, its `catalogue.variant` rows (`includeVariants`) and `catalogue.product_media` rows, and, with `includePrices`, one `catalogue.price` row per source price, on the same price list, for the matching new variant. A `code` already used by a product of the tenant (`product_code_uniq`, compared case-insensitively) is `409 duplicate-code` and nothing is written. The clone's `familyKey` is null: the family key is unique per venue (`product_venue_id_family_key_uniq`), so the source's cannot be copied.

**The same code rules as createProduct, a defined includePrices and a stated copy list** (ADAM CR-545; Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR545-001). (1) The body's `code` is exactly as `CreateProductRequest.code`: maxLength 64, pattern `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$`, unique per tenant compared ignoring case. A code already used by any product in the tenant answers `409 duplicate-code` (the shared DuplicateCode response), taken from the unique index on insert, not from a read first. A code that breaks the length or pattern answers `400 validation`. `name` is maxLength 200, as `Product.name`. (2) `includePrices` (default false): when true, for each source variant copied, every `catalogue.price` row of that variant (`price_list_id`, `list_price`, `tax_code_id`) is copied to the clone's matching new variant, on the same price list, with a new id, in the same transaction as the clone; CatalogueService owns the table. `includePrices` true with `includeVariants` false is refused with `400 validation` and nothing is written. Prices on price lists the caller cannot see at this venue are not copied. (3) **What a clone copies:** kind, venue, description, descriptionLocalised, category, responsible department, segment tags, channels, entitlement template, isStockTracked, onSaleFrom, onSaleTo, validTo, media rows (`catalogue.product_media`, pointing to the same assets), and, unless `includeVariants` is false, the variants under new ids with sku, barcode, axis values and names as stored. (4) **What a clone sets fresh:** new id; `code` and `name` from the body; `nameLocalised` null (`name` is the fallback, so guests never see the source's name); `familyKey` null (unique per venue); lifecycle `draft`, so `isSellable` false; `createdByPrincipalId` the caller; `approvedByPrincipalId` null; `createdAt` now; no versions, bundles, orders or other history. (5) **What a clone leaves out:** variant dimensions and asset usage records (VenueOps keeps those); staff add them after the clone. `fromPrice` in the `201` answer stays null. (6) **Tests:** a code already used in the tenant in different letter case gives `409 duplicate-code`; a code with a space gives `400`; `includePrices` true copies each price row to the matching new variant; `includePrices` with `includeVariants` false gives `400` and writes nothing; the clone's `nameLocalised` is null, `familyKey` null, `isSellable` false. The screen's clone form does not change.

### commitCatalogueImport

**`POST /products/import/{jobId}/commit`**: Apply a parsed catalogue import

1.4.2. The second phase. **An operator has seen the findings and accepted them** — two-phase exists so nobody commits 2,847 products sight-unseen.
Refuses a job whose `outcome` is `nothingFound` or `unreadable`. **A commit that applies nothing and reports success is the defect CF-122 fixed in `seating`**, and it is not being rebuilt here.

**The parse stages what it found and the commit applies exactly that** (ADAM CR-542; Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR542-001). It refuses these cases and changes nothing: a `jobKind` other than `productImport`, `409 catalogue-import-wrong-kind` (pricing jobs become change requests on ADM-080); status `parsing`, `committing` or `failed`, `409 catalogue-import-not-ready`; `committed`, `409 catalogue-import-already-committed`; `outcome` `nothingFound` or `unreadable`, or no row left to apply, `409 catalogue-import-found-nothing`; `replaceCategory` with live orders against the category, checked again, `409 category-has-live-orders`. Otherwise it moves the job to `committing` and applies the `pending` `catalogue.import_job_row` rows in `row_number` order, each in its own transaction, exactly as `createProduct` or `updateProduct` would, with their audit and product version. New products are `draft`; updated products keep their lifecycle state. Under `replaceCategory`, products of the category that are not in the file go from `live` to `withdrawn`, or from `draft` to `archived`. Each row ends `applied` (`product_id` set) or `failed` (`failure` set, plus a finding with its row, severity `error` and the message). At the end the job is `committed` if every row applied, and `failed` if any row failed (the partial application is recorded, never rolled back), with `applied_count`, `failed_count` and `completed_at` set. The answer is `200` with the job either way. The source file is never read again.

| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.audit_entry`, `catalogue.import_job`, `catalogue.import_job_row`, `catalogue.product`, `catalogue.product_version`, `catalogue.variant` |
| Called by | BO-117 |
| State model | Catalogue import job ([states/catalogue-import-job.yaml](../../../states/catalogue-import-job.yaml)): moves `previewReady` -> `committing` |

### getMyMemberships

**`GET /guests/me/memberships`**: A guest's own memberships, benefits and history

19.2.26 to 19.2.28. **`membership` is a `ProductKind`, so purchase and renewal already ride the order path — and nothing returned a guest their own membership.** No active list, no tier benefits, no prior terms.
Three requirements and one operation, because they are one screen: **what I have, what it gets me, and what I used to have.**
Includes lapsed terms. **A guest deciding whether to renew is comparing against what they had**, and a membership that vanishes on expiry makes that impossible.

**The guest's read** (ADAM CR-543): it stays the guest's read, as the 2 October decision binds it on WEB-022, WEB-023 and GST-015; `listGuestMemberships` stays for staff and builds `GuestMembership` with the same code and rules.

**GuestMembership, field by field** (ADAM CR-543; Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR543-001). Rows: the signed-in guest's `access.entitlement` rows (`subject_id` is the guest) whose product's kind is `membership`. A chain is the guest's entitlements of one product plus any linked by `supersedes_entitlement_id`; the newest in a chain is the row, and the earlier ones are its `previousTerms`. A surrendered entitlement (transferred away) is not shown. `includeLapsed=false` leaves out rows whose `status` is `expired` or `cancelled`; true (the default) keeps them. Order: `validFrom` ascending, then `entitlementId`. Benefits are read from `catalogue.plan_benefit` and `catalogue.membership_benefit`; Marketing is not called (`tier` is null). `GuestMembership` states each field's rule.

### joinWaitlist

**`POST /waitlist-entries`**: Ask to be told if capacity frees up

1.1.25, 4.9.17. **Offered only where the performance is genuinely sold out** — a waitlist on something available is a worse experience than selling it.
**Sold out counts held seats and live leases as sold** (decided 28 September, audit R101). A performance is sold out when sold units, seats on hold and units under an unexpired lease together reach its capacity; the oversell allowance does not reopen it. (A "session" in the design is a Performance, audit R165.)
**Joining reserves nothing.** The guest is told when capacity appears and buys like anyone else, because holding capacity for a waitlist is the same as not releasing it.

### listProductCategories

**`GET /product-categories`**: The merchandise hierarchy — categories, brands, collections

Retail Board 2. **`listSeatCategories` existed and a product category did not** — a seat category prices a seat; this groups a catalogue.
**Returned as a tree, not a flat list with parent ids.** A merchandiser reading a hierarchy five levels deep should not have to reassemble it, and every caller that does will reassemble it differently.
**Whose tree, and what a guest sees** (R8 blocker fix, GPS-C01 and WHITE-C05). The tree is the venue's: `venueId` names it for a caller with no venue (a visitor, an app before sign-in), as `listProducts` does, and is left out by a caller who acts at one. **A guest or visitor gets only active categories of `kind` `category`** (not `brand`, `collection`, `season` or `department`), at every depth, unless the screen asks for a kind: `kind` narrows the tree to that kind. Staff see every kind and the inactive ones, as before.

**How the tree is built when a node is dropped** (ADAM CR-523; Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR523-001): inactive nodes are pruned with their whole subtree; a node dropped only for its kind passes its kept descendants up. The answer is built in two passes over the venue's `catalogue.product_category` rows. **Pass 1, for a guest or visitor only:** drop every inactive node together with everything below it, whatever the kinds below, so a section a venue switches off hides all that is filed under it. Staff skip pass 1 and see inactive nodes. **Pass 2, for every caller:** the kind in force is the `kind` parameter; with no `kind` parameter it is `category` for a guest or visitor, and every kind for staff (so nothing is dropped). Remove each remaining node whose kind is not the one in force, and attach each kept child to the nearest kept ancestor, or make it a root when no ancestor is kept. Among the new siblings, order by `displayOrder` ascending, then by `id`. A node never appears under a node it is not filed beneath. Contract tests: a category under a department appears as a root for a guest; a brand under a category answers `kind=brand` with the brand as a root; an active category under an inactive one is absent for a guest and present for staff.

| Scope level | venue |

| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Read routing | replica |
| Guest callable | True |
| Reads | `catalogue.product`, `catalogue.product_category` |
| Writes | - |
| Called by | BO-048, BO-115, CMS-101, CMS-103, GST-001, GST-002, GST-007, GST-008, WEB-002, WEB-005 |

**Parameters**

| kind | query |  | enum (category, brand, collection, season, department) | Only categories of this kind (category, brand, collection, season or department). |

|---|---|---|

### searchCatalogue

**`GET /search`**: Find something by name

**The guest app browses by category and cannot search.** On a venue with two hundred products that is the difference between finding a thing and giving up.
**Where Book goes: `bookingEntry` and `bundleId`** (ADAM CR-591, Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR591-001). On a guest or visitor read every answered product carries the two read-only fields, worked out on read and never stored; both are null on staff reads. `Product.bookingEntry` states the rule. The flow rule is WhiteLabel's, asked once per page through `white-label#publishedBookingFlowSteps` (service route), the way the published booking settings are read from white-label; Catalogue never copies the rule, and a product that does not resolve gets the kind fallback, never an error for the page.
Distinct from `semanticSearch`, which is AI and retrieves from documents. This is a keyword search over the sellable catalogue, and it works with no AI configured — a venue without an assistant still needs a search box.
Scoped to what is on sale at the venue and channel, so a guest never finds a product they cannot buy, **with one exception: information-only products** (decided 29 September, rev 3 REV3-14). A product with `guestListing: infoOnly` is returned whether or not it is on sale, flagged with its `guestListing` and `notBookableLabel`, so a guest finds it, reads it and is told it cannot be booked online; the result opens the product's details and never offers to add it to the basket. The old rule *a guest never finds what they cannot buy* still holds for `bookable` products and is superseded for `infoOnly` ones. A `hidden` product is never returned to a guest. When the venue's `BookingFlowConfig.showInfoOnly` is off, `infoOnly` products are left out as well.
**Help me choose narrows the search too** (decided 29 September, W4): `guidedAnswerIds` applies the chosen answers' filters, exactly as on `listProducts`.

**What it matches, answers and reads** (ADAM CR-533, option A; Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR533-001). (1) `summary` is the product's description (`catalogue.product.description`); no summary column is added. Each result also carries `nameLocalised` and `summaryLocalised` (`catalogue.product.name_localised` and `description_localised`); the screen shows the guest's language and falls back to `name` and `summary`. (2) The optional `channel` sets both the on-sale scope and `fromPrice`. (3) `showInfoOnly` is read through `white-label#tenantLanguageRecords` (service) for the tenant's current published configuration version: `BookingFlowConfig` from its snapshot, with the venue's `venueOverrides` entry laid over field by field; when the setting is absent it is on. When it is off, `infoOnly` products are left out. (4) The swim rule: a product's questions are `catalogue.product.consent_question_ids`; `marketing-crm#consentQuestionRecords` (service) is asked once per page for the ids the candidate products name, and when an answer has `requiresSwimmer` false, any product with a question of kind `swim` is left out. (5) `fromPrice` calls the one price rule, `catalogue#variantPriceRecords` (ADAM CR-439, built once in Catalogue on #35288; called, never copied), reading `catalogue.price_list` and `catalogue.price`. `listProducts` declares the same reads for (3) and (4) and applies the same rules.

### setChannelConnectionConfiguration

**`PUT /channel-connections/{connectionId}`**: Create or update a channel connection

**How TICVAI reaches one channel, per environment** (29 September, writers pass). Creates or updates a `catalogue.channel_connection`, keyed by `salesChannelId`, `connectorName` and `environment`. **Credentials are never sent or stored here**: `credentialsReference` and `certificateReference` name secrets in the vault (`422` `secretNotReference` if a value is not a vault reference). A new or changed connection is saved `notTested`; `testChannelConnection` moves it to `connected` or `degraded`/`offline`. `disabled` switches it off. An existing OTA adapter is enabled for a venue by naming its `adapterId`, with no rebuild (MoM 31 Aug 2026 §4.3). Written to `catalogue.audit_entry` (`domain: channel`).

**What a vault reference is** (ADAM CR-459, decided by Chinmay on 2 November 2026, option A: one format, checked in one place). `credentialsReference` and `certificateReference`, when not null, are checked with the kernel's shared vault-reference validator, which takes the caller's tenant id and the value: the value must be the name of a secret in the cell's Key Vault namespaced to the tenant, matching `^t-<tenantId>-[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9-]{0,78}[a-z0-9])?$`, `<tenantId>` the caller's tenant id as a lowercase uuid with dashes (at most 119 characters). Anything else is `422 secret-not-reference`: `errors[]` names the field, nothing is stored, and the refused value is never echoed in the problem, logged or written to `catalogue.audit_entry`, because it may be a secret. The save does not check that the secret exists, since it may be put in the vault later; `testChannelConnection`'s authentication test checks that it resolves. Reads keep returning the reference: it is a name, not a secret.

| Part of slice | setup, makes `catalogue.channel_connection` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Config scope | venue |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Read routing | primary |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.channel_connection`, `catalogue.sales_channel` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.audit_entry`, `catalogue.channel_connection` |
| Called by | ADM-269 |

### setChannelSalesRule

**`PUT /channel-sales-rules/{ruleId}`**: Create or replace a channel sales window, limit or eligibility rule

**One rule on a channel** (29 September, writers pass). Creates or replaces a `catalogue.channel_sales_rule`: a sales window (`ruleKind: salesWindow`), a sales limit or restriction (`salesLimit`) or a customer eligibility rule (`eligibility`), for the whole channel or one product (`productId`), with an effective window. Only the fields of its `ruleKind` are kept; the others are cleared. A rule is switched off with `isActive: false`, never deleted, so what applied to a past sale can be shown. Written to `catalogue.audit_entry` (`domain: channel`).

Rules: `effectiveTo` before `effectiveFrom`, or `minimumQuantity` above `maximumQuantity`, is refused (`422` `invalidRange`); an `eligibility` rule needs `eligibilityDimension`, `eligibilityOperator` and `eligibilityEffect` (`422` `eligibilityIncomplete`); a product-level rule on a product that already has one for the same `ruleKind` and window needs `overridesProductRule` (`409` `ruleConflict`).

**When a save conflicts** (ADAM CR-466, decided by Chinmay on 2 November 2026: option A as built, with two corrections from earlier decisions). Saving an active rule (`isActive` true) that has a `productId` and `overridesProductRule` false is refused with `409 rule-conflict`, and nothing is stored, when another active `catalogue.channel_sales_rule` (not the one being replaced) has the same `sales_channel_id`, `product_id` and `rule_kind` and an overlapping effective window. `ruleLevel` plays no part: CR-411 already reads a product-level rule as a row with a product. Windows are instants: a null `effective_from` means from when that rule was saved (its `created_at`, or now for the rule being saved), not open to the past (CR-414); a null `effective_to` is open-ended; two windows overlap when each starts before the other ends. A save with `isActive` false never conflicts, so a rule can always be switched off, and `overridesProductRule` true lets the save through. Before the check the save takes a transaction lock on the sales channel, product and rule kind (`pg_advisory_xact_lock` on a hash of the three), so two saves at once cannot both pass. The problem's `detail` names the id of the rule it collides with. A rule with no `productId` never gets this 409; `listChannelSaleSchedule`'s conflicts count (CR-411) still shows a channel-wide rule overlapping a product rule.

### setFeeDefinition

**`PUT /fees/{feeId}`**: Create or update a fee or surcharge

**The fee and surcharge library** (29 September, writers pass). Creates or updates a `catalogue.fee`, keyed by `code`: what the fee is and how it computes. When it applies is a `catalogue.fee_rule` (`setFeeApplicabilityCharging`). Distinct from a provider's processing cost (`payments.fee_rule`) and from a fee as charged on an order (`orders.order_fee`).

Rules: `fixedAmount` needs `amount`, `percentage` needs `percentage`, `tiered` needs `tiers` (`422` `valueRequired`). **A `draft` fee is saved directly; changing one already `active` is refused (`409` `changeRequestRequired`)** and goes through `setPricingChangeRequest`. Written to `catalogue.audit_entry` (`domain: pricing`).

### setMembershipBenefit

**`PUT /membership-benefits/{benefitId}`**: Define a benefit

|  |  |
|---|---|

### setPlanBenefits

**`PUT /entitlement-templates/{templateId}/benefits`**: Replace the benefits a plan grants

**Set whole.** A plan losing one benefit and gaining another is one change to a guest, and applying it in two steps sells a plan that briefly has neither or both.

### setProductCategories

**`PUT /product-categories`**: Define the hierarchy, in the order a guest sees it

**The whole tree in one call.** A hierarchy edited node by node is a hierarchy that spends time in states nobody intended — a category reparented before its new parent exists.
**Refuses a cycle**, and refuses deleting a category that names products: **a category with a season behind it still names what was sold under it**, and removing it rewrites last year's report. Deactivate instead.
**What the PUT does.** The body is the venue's whole hierarchy, flat, each category naming its parent by `parentId`, and matched on `id`: a category whose `id` is new is created, one whose `id` exists is replaced by what is sent, and one left out of the body is deleted. Leaving out a category that products name is the deletion refused above — send it with `isActive: false`.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Config scope | venue |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.product`, `catalogue.product_category`, `whitelabel.booking_flow` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.product_category` |
| Called by | BO-115, CMS-103 |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Request body**

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| categories | array of ProductCategory | yes |  |
| categories[].id | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| categories[].name | string | yes |  |
| categories[].code | string |  | Taken from their category tables, 20 September. (max length 64; nullable) |
| categories[].nameLocalised | object |  |  |
| categories[].kind | enum (category, brand, collection, season, department) | yes |  |
| categories[].parentId | string (uuid) |  | One tree, not four. (nullable) |
| categories[].scopePath | string |  | Set by the server from the venue the caller acts at; not sent. (read-only) |
| categories[].displayOrder | integer |  | (default 100) |
| categories[].imageAssetId | string (uuid) |  | (nullable) |
| categories[].description | object |  | The short line a guest reads under a category option, e.g. (nullable) |
| categories[].bookingFlowId | string (uuid) |  | The booking flow for every product filed here that names none of its own (decided 29 September, W12, BO-115). (nullable) |
| categories[].isActive | boolean |  | Deactivated rather than deleted. (default True) |

**Responses**

| Code | Shape | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 200 |  | Set |
| 400 |  | The body contains a cycle — a category that is its own ancestor — or a parentId that names no category in the body. |
| 409 |  | The body leaves out a category that products name (send it with isActive false rather than deleting it), or a category code is already used in this tenant, in the body or by another venue's category… |
| 422 |  | A category bookingFlowId that is not a booking flow of the venue (W12, 29 September). |
| 429 | TooManyRequests | Rate limit exceeded for this tenant, venue or principal. |

### updateSeatCategory

**`PATCH /seat-categories/{seatCategoryId}`**: Rename, re-rank or re-price a seat category

Added with `SeatCategory.priceBands` (decided 28 September, audit R275 (d)), so BO-1045 can change a category's bands after it is created. `priceBands`, when sent, replaces the whole list; a band left out is removed. ### generateCouponCodes

**`POST /coupon-campaigns/{campaignId}/codes`**: Generate codes in bulk

Up to fifty thousand at a time. Generation is asynchronous and the batch is exported rather than returned inline — a hundred thousand codes is a file, not a response body.
**Poll `getCouponCodeBatch` with the returned `batchId`** for progress, the failure reason and, once `complete`, the export's download link. `listCouponCodes` filters by the same `batchId`.
**Codes are single-use by default** (the campaign's `isSingleUse` defaults to true), and **the number of assigned guests must not exceed `quantity`**. Fewer is allowed: the codes left over are anonymous (decided 28 September, audit R101).
**Writes `promotions.promotion_audit`** in the same transaction, as every promotions configuration change does: one row per batch, not per code (entityType `couponCampaign`, eventType `modified`, newValue the batch id and quantity) (decided 29 September, writers pass).

| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `promotions.coupon_campaign` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `promotions.coupon_code`, `promotions.coupon_code_batch`, `promotions.promotion_audit` |
| Called by | ADM-160, BO-010 |

**Parameters**

| assignToSubjectIds | array of string (uuid) |  | Personalised codes bound to named guests. (max items 50000) |

| quantity | integer |  |  |

| 429 | TooManyRequests | Rate limit exceeded for this tenant, venue or principal. |

### getGuestCouponCode

**`GET /guest-coupon-codes/{code}`**: What a guest is told about a coupon code

A guest's check of a code before applying it (R8 blocker ORDERS-MONEY-C32, the lead's answer A of 20 October 2026; GST-037). Answers `GuestCouponCode`: the code, its status, whether it can be applied now and why not, the discount and the validity window, never its campaign, batch, assignee, assignment, counts or the order it was redeemed on. `getCouponCode` stays the staff read and is unchanged.
**The rule, on what is stored** (a coupon campaign has `is_active`, `valid_from` and `valid_to` and no link to a promotion): an unknown code is 404. A known code is 200, and when it cannot be applied `invalidReason` is `expired`, `notYetValid`, `voided` (also when the campaign is not active), `alreadyRedeemed` (the guest's own redemption), `notAssignedToGuest` (assigned to someone else, without saying to whom) or `wrongVenue` (only when `venueId` is sent and the code is not valid there). A guest needs no permission; the code is what the guest holds, and the existing `429` limits stop guessing.

| Writes | - |

## Group: drafted

### createChannelProfile

**`POST /channel-profile`**: Channel Creation & Profile Configuration

**Drafted from the workshop pack; the shape below is read out of it, not invented.** Sales Channel Management, page 5. The screen says: Create and define a sales channel before products and commercial rules are assigned.

**Agreed (decided 29 September, readiness close-out).** the eight scope options became scopeLevel + scopeId; 'may require' prose and allocation rules dropped (allocation lives on ADM-262); type-specific settings typed as IDs/lists; channelType enum, currency/country/code patterns; tenant comes from context; response carries the new channelId and draft status. Save creates the channel in draft; the type-specific fields (POS: venue, workstation groups, cashier access; B2C: webstore, domain, journey; OTA: partner, API connection) are required by readiness validation, not by this call.

**Code, scope, audit, type and currency** (ADAM CR-413, decided 31 October 2026, Chinmay). (1) The code is unique within the tenant, case-insensitively (the unique index `sales_channel_code_uniq` on `catalogue.sales_channel (lower(code))`): the call answers `409 duplicate-code` (the shared `DuplicateCode`) by mapping the unique violation on that index, so a code held at a venue the caller cannot see is still refused. (2) `scopeId` is a uuid: the region, venue, attraction, event, location or business unit the channel is scoped to. For `global` and `country` it is empty, and a country channel names its country in `country`. A `scopeId` that is not a uuid, or one sent for `global` or `country`, is `400 validation` on `scopeId`; `country` scope without `country` is `400 validation` on `country`. (3) The audit row is `catalogue.audit_entry` with domain `channel`, action `created`, entity_type `salesChannel`, and `entity_id` and `sales_channel_id` the new channel's id. (4) `channelType` `kiosk` maps to `salesChannel` `kiosk`. (5) `currency` is not stored, because a channel sells in the currency its venue trades in (ADR-0018): it stays in the input only so older clients validate, is checked against its pattern and dropped.

| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Read routing | primary |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.sales_channel` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.audit_entry`, `catalogue.sales_channel` |
| Called by | ADM-259 |

### publishChannelAvailability

**`PUT /channel-availability`**: Channel Publication & Availability

**Drafted from the workshop pack; the shape below is read out of it, not invented.** Product Lifecycle   Catalogue Governance, page 9. The screen says: Control where a product may be exposed for sale.

**Agreed (decided 29 September, readiness close-out).** enable/disable became a typed per-channel list ($ref Channel) with sites, POS groups, venues and effective dates; preview and missing dependencies are response-only (publicationPreview, validationIssues); productId and the count of issued entitlements that stay valid added. Removing a channel stops future sale only; previously issued tickets stay valid, and the response shows issuedEntitlementsUnaffected so ADM-124 can say so. Only approved or live products can be exposed; channel configuration itself belongs to Sales Channel Management.

**A PUT replaces the product's publication whole, so a caller reads it first** (20 October 2026, R8 blocker cause GPS-C39; CHG-R8SPN-038). Every screen that changes one part (BO-291 its channels, ADM-124 everything) reads the current publication with `getChannelAvailability`, changes what it shows, and sends the rest back as read: `siteIds`, `posGroupIds`, `venueIds` and the dates it does not show are kept only because they are sent. A body that leaves them out clears them.

**Membership channels map onto catalogue channels** (GPS-C39): the channel values BO-291's membership view carries (subscription) map `guestWeb` -> `web`, `guestApp` -> `mobile`, `Reseller` (or `partner`) -> `b2b`, and the rest by the same name where one exists; `api` has no catalogue channel (public API sale is governed by the API licensing, not by this list) and is not sent.

### publishChannelReadinessValidation

**`PUT /channel-readiness-validation`**: Channel Publication, Readiness & AI Validation

**Drafted from the workshop pack; the shape below is read out of it, not invented.** Sales Channel Management, page 16. The screen says: Perform final validation before a sales channel or channel/product configuration becomes commercially active.

**Agreed (decided 29 September, readiness close-out).** 17 fragments of the checklist became a request (channel, action, schedule, override reason) and a response (readiness score, per-area checklist, severity-graded issues, advisory AI findings, resulting status). Actions Validate, Preview, Submit for Approval, Schedule Activation, Activate, Return for Changes and Suspend on ADM-267 are this PUT's action; a critical issue blocks activation, warnings pass only with the override permission and a mandatory reason; preview returns the effective Product -> Channel -> Customer -> Price -> Capacity -> Payment -> Fulfillment chain without activating.

**Approval, override, refusals and the timer** (ADAM CR-211, option A, decided 25 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-003). The moves are `states/sales-channel.yaml`; the view's `status` is its five values. `submitForApproval` (draft -> pendingApproval) raises an approval request through `approvals.createApprovalRequest` (kind `configurationChange`, `subjectType` `salesChannel`, `subjectContract` `catalogue`, `subjectId` the channel) and answers its id as `approvalRequestId`. `scheduleActivation` and `activate` from `pendingApproval` take that `approvalRequestId`, and are refused `409 approval-required` unless it names an approved request for this channel (read with `approvals#requestRecords`). **Warnings** pass only with `overrideWarnings` from a holder of `SALES_CHANNEL_OVERRIDE` (`x-ticvai-permission-escalated`; 403 without it) and a `reason`; a critical issue is never overridden. **Refusals**: a move the state model does not allow (for example `activate` from `draft`) is `409 invalid-channel-transition`; a blocking critical issue is `422 critical-issue-blocks-activation`; a missing reason (overriding warnings, returning for changes, suspending) is `422 reason-required`; a missing or past `scheduledAt` on `scheduleActivation` is `422 scheduled-at-invalid`. Nothing changes on a refusal. **preview** carries the effective chain in the view's `preview`, and changes nothing. **The scheduled activation** is run by the channel activation job in the workers host: at `activation_scheduled_at` it moves a `scheduled` channel to `active` when readiness still has no critical issue, and otherwise leaves it `scheduled` and writes the issue to `catalogue.audit_entry` (result `failure`).

|---|---|
| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | setup, makes `catalogue.sales_channel` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Read routing | primary |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.channel_capacity`, `catalogue.channel_connection`, `catalogue.channel_envelope`, `catalogue.channel_sales_rule`, `catalogue.price_assignment`, `catalogue.price_list`, `catalogue.product`, `catalogue.product_channel_assignment`, `catalogue.sales_channel` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.audit_entry`, `catalogue.sales_channel` |
| Called by | ADM-267 |
| State model | Sales channel ([states/sales-channel.yaml](../../../states/sales-channel.yaml)): moves `draft` -> `pendingApproval`, `pendingApproval` -> `draft`, `pendingApproval` -> `scheduled`, `pendingApproval` -> `active`, `active` -> `suspended`, `suspended` -> `active` |

### setChannelFeePayment

**`PUT /channel-fee-payment`**: Channel Fees, Payment & Fulfillment Configuration

**Drafted from the workshop pack; the shape below is read out of it, not invented.** Sales Channel Management, page 14. The screen says: Define the commercial and fulfillment behavior associated with each channel.

**Agreed (decided 29 September, readiness close-out).** six fee amounts became fee-profile references (the Fee Engine owns amounts); 7 payment and 11 fulfilment fields became two enum lists; the per-channel example fields dropped; print-at-home, voucher, API delivery and bulk CSV added as fulfilment methods; validationIssues added. Saving exposes only methods approved in Payment and Ticket Media; an incompatible combination is reported in validationIssues and blocks readiness.

**How each validation issue is found** (ADAM CR-212, option A, decided 25 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-004). **paymentMethodNotApproved**: a method in `paymentMethods` (by kind: `card`, `cash`, `digitalWallet` and `paymentLink` as named, `accountCredit` as `storedValue`, `b2bCredit` as `onAccount`) or in `otherPaymentMethodCodes` (by code) that is not among the channel's venue's approved methods, asked of the payments module with `payments#approvedPaymentMethods` in process. **mediaProfileMissing**: an `rfid`, `nfc` or `wristband` fulfilment method with no active `access.media_encoding_profile` for the channel's venue, read in process with `access#mediaEncodingProfileRecords` (a granted read, XDR-002). **feeProfileMissing**: each `fees[].feeType` maps to `catalogue.fee.fee_type` (`booking` -> `bookingFee`, `transaction` -> `transactionFee`, `channel` -> `channelFee`, `service` -> `serviceFee`, `delivery` -> `deliveryFee`, `payment` -> `paymentFee`), and a referenced `feeProfileId` must name a `catalogue.fee` that exists, is of that type and is `active` (`CatalogueConfigStatus`); otherwise `feeProfileMissing`. The issues are answered in `validationIssues`; the configuration is still saved, and readiness is blocked until they are fixed.

### setCodeDistributionManager

**`PUT /code-distribution-manager`**: Code Distribution & Assignment Manager

**Drafted from the workshop pack; the shape below is read out of it, not invented.** Promotions   Bundles Management, page 42. The screen says: Manage how promotional codes are allocated and distributed.

**Every property carries the sentence it came from.** 26 were read from the screen's own bulleted directory and 4 bullets were dropped as prose, examples or hierarchy illustrations rather than bent into fields. Names and types are this package's reading of those sentences and are the part to check; the sentences themselves are the client's.

**Agreed 29 September (decided 29 September, readiness close-out).** Checked against the pack page and the minutes: no promotions minutes on file (the 3 September walkthrough was not minuted), so our build plan. Shape: 6 properties removed that were filters, actions, behaviour, roles, examples or prose fragments rather than fields; 11 one-value-per-field properties folded into enums (assigneeType); added batchId, partner; renamed viewedWhereAvailable to viewed.

Outbound sending runs through the CRM/marketing communication services, not a second messaging engine here.

**Where it is stored** (4 October 2026, CHG-FXC-005). The input becomes one `promotions.code_assignment` row
(CouponCodeAssignment: channelsType, assigneeType, assigneeReference, batchId, quantity); `quantity` codes of the batch
that are `issued` and unassigned move to `assigned` with `assignmentId` set and `distributionStatus` `pending`
(`409 not-enough-codes-in-batch` when the batch has fewer left, naming how many are left; nothing is written).
Every PUT adds an assignment this way (ADAM CR-316); none replaces an earlier one. The view counts the batch's codes: `generated` all of them, `assigned` those with an
assignment, `sent`, `delivered`, `viewed` and `cancelled` by `distributionStatus`, `redeemed` and `expired` by
`status`.

## Group: entitlement

### createEntitlementTemplate

**`POST /entitlement-templates`**: Create an entitlement template

**Where the template is written** (ADAM CR-217, option A, decided 25 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-001). `catalogue.entitlement_template.scope_path` is set by the server, never taken from the body: it is the path of the caller's working venue (the session's venue id), read with `tenancy#scopeRecords` by id. Catalogue never reads `platform.scope` raw (ADR-0055 rule 2 as amended). **A session with no working venue** (a head-office or region caller) is `403 working-venue-required`, and nothing is written; giving such a caller a venue in the body waits for its own decision.

**includedValue** is common Money, and the column keeps the amount only (ADR-0018; naming-and-style 5.1): its currency and scale are the venue's (`tenancy#venueSettingsRecords`, by the working venue's id). A request whose `includedValue.currency` or `scale` differs from the venue's is `400 venue-currency-mismatch` or `venue-scale-mismatch`, and nothing is written. The 201 answers includedValue in the venue's currency and scale.

|  |  |

| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | setup, makes `catalogue.entitlement_template` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Config scope | venue |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.entitlement_template` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.entitlement_template` |
| Called by | BO-012, BO-288 |

### setEntitlementTemplateBlackoutDates

**`PUT /entitlement-templates/{templateId}/blackout-dates`**: Set the dates a product's entitlement does not admit

**Blackouts from the closures screen** (design-notes correction on BO-019, Block B: "No operation sets product blackout dates or a venue closure day; the screen only cancels performances and closes paths. Blackouts live on entitlement templates (blackoutDates) and are edited on BO-012; a closures screen needs a write for them"; DI-452; CHG-CSP-051). Replaces `EntitlementTemplate.blackoutDates` of one template, which until now could be set only when the template was created. An annual pass that excludes public holidays is the usual case. Applies to entitlements issued from the template from now on and to existing ones at their next validation; a date already admitted on is not undone. A whole-venue closure day is the access operating calendar's (`setOperatingCalendarEntry` with `venueClosed`).

**includedValue in the answer** (ADAM CR-235, option A with CR-217, decided 25 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-001). The 200 is the whole template, and its includedValue is answered in its venue's currency and scale: the template keeps only its `scope_path`, so Catalogue asks `tenancy#scopeRecords` by that path for the venue node, then `tenancy#venueSettingsRecords` by the venue's id. It is never guessed from the caller's working venue, and a template with includedValue is answered, not refused.

**The audit row** (confirmed as built, ADAM CR-235): one `catalogue.audit_entry` with domain `product`, action `updated`, category `validity`, entity_type `entitlementTemplate`, entity id the template's, field `blackoutDates`, previous_value and new_value the two lists as JSON arrays, `reason` the body's, scope_path the template's, and actor_principal_id the caller.

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `PRODUCT_CONFIGURE` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | setup, makes `catalogue.entitlement_template` non-empty |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Config scope | venue |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.entitlement_template` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.audit_entry`, `catalogue.entitlement_template` |
| Called by | **no screen**: no screen lists it in its apis, so it is reachable only by API or import until one does (README, Known gaps) |

## Group: evaluation

### evaluatePromotions

**`POST /promotions/evaluate`**: Evaluate promotions against a cart

Returns the applied discount **and the working** — which rules matched, which did not, and why. A cashier facing a guest who expected a discount needs an answer, not a total.
Terminals evaluate locally from the catalogue bundle. This endpoint serves online channels and lets the back office test a rule before publishing it.
**Reads `promotions.promotion_rule`**: a promotion whose `conditions.eligibilityRuleIds` names reusable eligibility rules is eligible only when each named rule holds (decided 29 September, writers pass).
**Writes `promotions.promotion_evaluation_trace` only when it prices an order being confirmed.** The order service calls it with `orderId` when it prices an order for payment (createOrder, convertReservation); that call stores one trace row for the order: which promotions were evaluated, which were eligible, which applied in what order and why the others did not. A cart, till or back-office evaluation sends no `orderId` and stores nothing (decided 29 September, writers pass).
**A guest does this within their own session** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Pattern 4; CHG-GCF-003; `x-ticvai-guest-callable`): a guest needs no permission and acts only for themselves, on their own cart, hold, order or payment in their own session. `PRICE_VIEW` is what a staff caller must hold to do it for a guest at a till or in the back office (ADR-0025).

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Permission | `PRICE_VIEW` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | yes |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Guest callable | True |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency`, `catalogue.price_list`, `promotions.coupon_code`, `promotions.promotion`, `promotions.promotion_rule` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency`, `promotions.promotion_evaluation_trace` |
| Called by | ADM-157, ADM-177, BO-010, POS-002, POS-021, POS-023, PTR-010, WEB-005, WEB-010 |
| State model | Coupon code ([states/coupon.yaml](../../../states/coupon.yaml)): moves `issued` -> `redeemed`, `assigned` -> `redeemed` |

### createEvent

**`POST /events`**: Create an event

**`parentEventId`, the group an event belongs to** (accepted 26 October 2026, Chinmay, ADAM CR-256 option A). It must name an existing event the caller can see, at the same venue; not the event itself; and one level only: a parent has no parent, and an event that is a parent cannot be given one. Otherwise `400 validation` on `parentEventId`. The key `catalogue.event.parent_event_id` -> `catalogue.event` is declared.

### enterWaitingRoom

**`POST /performances/{performanceId}/waiting-room/entries`**: Join the on-sale waiting room for a performance

**Arrival costs a Redis increment and nothing else** (ADR-0066, accepted 1 October). The guest gets a position from the performance's counter in Azure Managed Redis (ADR-0032's product amendment) and an entry id to poll with. **No database write, no session, no personal data**: the guest may not be signed in yet, and the waiting page is static and branded, served from Front Door's cache.

When the performance's room is off, the answer is `state: notRequired` and the guest goes straight to the sale. A second call with the same `Idempotency-Key` returns the same entry, so a refresh does not send the guest to the back.

**Not `queue.joinQueue`.** That is the ride queue (Q1) and writes `queue.entry` per guest; the waiting parts of WEB-015 and GST-046 move here (ADR-0066).

|---|---|
| Permission | `None` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | `cache:idempotency` |
| Writes | `cache:idempotency` |
| Called by | GST-046, WEB-015 |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| performanceId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| Idempotency-Key | header | yes | string (uuid) | Client-generated UUIDv7. |

**Response**: `WaitingRoomEntry`

| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| waitingEntryId | string (uuid) | yes | A UUIDv7 minted on arrival; what the waiting page polls with. |
| performanceId | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| state | enum (notRequired, waiting, admitted, expired, closed) | yes | notRequired: the room is off, go to the sale. |
| position | integer |  | Place in the room; null unless waiting. (min 1; nullable) |
| aheadOfYou | integer |  | (min 0; nullable) |
| estimatedWaitSeconds | integer |  | At the current release rate. (min 0; nullable) |
| pollAfterSeconds | integer |  | (min 1; default 5) |
| admission | object |  | Set when state is admitted. (nullable) |
| admission.token | string | yes | The signed token, opaque to the client. |
| admission.tokenId | string (uuid) |  | The token's unique id. |
| admission.performanceId | string (uuid) | yes |  |
| admission.expiresAt | string (date-time) | yes |  |

**Responses**

| Code | Shape | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 201 |  | In the room, with a position; or notRequired when the room is off |
| 404 | NotFound | The resource does not exist, or is outside the caller's scope. |
| 429 | TooManyRequests | Rate limit exceeded for this tenant, venue or principal. |

### getPerformance

**`GET /performances/{performanceId}`**: Read a performance

**A guest calls this without a permission and sees published data only** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Pattern 4; CHG-GCF-001; `x-ticvai-guest-callable`): a guest or a visitor gets a published performance, and nothing in draft, unpublished or withdrawn; asked for by id, such a record answers as not found. `PRODUCT_VIEW` is what a staff caller must hold, and staff also see drafts (ADR-0025).

**Published follows the event's lifecycle** (ADAM CR-111, option 1, accepted 15 October 2026; CHG-R10CAT-011): a performance is published when its event's `lifecycleState` is `onSale` or `live`, as `listEvents` shows a guest; `Performance.status` has no draft value and does not decide it. Any other performance answers a guest 404.

| Writes | - |

### getWaitingRoomPosition

**`GET /performances/{performanceId}/waiting-room/entries/{waitingEntryId}`**: Where a waiting guest is, and the admission token once admitted

**Polled by the waiting page, answered from the edge's cache** for a few seconds at a time (`pollAfterSeconds` says when to ask again). Reads the Redis counters only.

**Admitted, it carries the admission token** (`WaitingRoomAdmission`): short-lived and signed, naming the tenant, the performance, its expiry and a unique id; the signing key is a Key Vault secret. The client sends it as `X-Admission-Token` on `orders.addCartLine`, which forwards it to `acquireInventoryHold`; both refuse a missing or invalid token `403 admission-required` for a performance whose room is on. The admitted answer is never cached at the edge.

**On the guest web the token is also an HttpOnly, short-lived, per-performance cookie** on the admitted answer (ADR-0066 amendment of 25 October 2026, ADAM CR-216): the browser sends it with `addCartLine` and the page stores nothing, so a reload or a second tab stays admitted. **The API itself sends the cookie** on the admitted answer (ADAM CR-524, Chinmay, 3 November 2026; ADR-0066 amendment of 3 November 2026): `Set-Cookie`, HttpOnly, Secure, scoped to the performance, expiring with the token. Front Door passes it through and never caches the admitted answer. **A `404` for a performance the caller knows means the entry is gone** (evicted, or the room restarted): the guest re-enters with `enterWaitingRoom`.

**Built with the waiting room, on #29033** (ADAM CR-524, Chinmay, 3 November 2026; CHG-CR524-001), with `enterWaitingRoom`, the release controller and the token check, as that ticket lists; not on the catalogue event ticket. #29033 settles in its own design, before building, everything both operations and the cart check share: the per-performance Redis keys (the arrival counter and the admitted-up-to mark the controller advances), the per-entry record and its lifetime, the token (format, signing algorithm, Key Vault secret name, lifetime), the rules for `position`, `aheadOfYou`, `estimatedWaitSeconds` and `pollAfterSeconds`, and when an entry is expired or the room closed rather than `notRequired`.

|---|---|
| Permission | `None` |
| Scope level | venue |
| Part of slice | core |
| Wave | 1 |
| Offline | no |
| Conflict policy | serverWins |
| Reads | - |
| Writes | - |
| Called by | GST-046, WEB-015 |

**Parameters**

| Name | In | Required | Type | Notes |

| performanceId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |
| waitingEntryId | path | yes | string (uuid) |  |

### listEvents

**`GET /events`**: List events

**The venue's programme, for a guest too** (decided 20 October 2026, Chinmay, R8 blocker cause GPS-C30, option A; CHG-R8SPN-034). GST-005 What's On lists the venue's events from here and then each event's performances (`listPerformances`). **A guest or a visitor calls this without a permission and sees published data only** (Pattern 4; CHG-GCF-001; `x-ticvai-guest-callable`): events in `onSale` or `live` that are active (`isActive`), and nothing in `draft`, `planned`, `closed`, `cancelled` or `archived`. A visitor with no credential sends `venueId`. `PRODUCT_VIEW` is what a staff caller must hold, and staff see every state.

**Order.** Sorted by `name` ascending, then by `id`. **Statuses.** For staff every `lifecycleState`; for a guest only `onSale` and `live`, as above.

**`venueId` and `X-Venue-Id` name the same thing** (ADAM CR-305 G3, option A, Chinmay, 29 October 2026; CHG-CR305-001). Either names the venue; both sent and different is `400 unknown-venue`. An unknown venue, from either, is `400 unknown-venue` (not the header's usual `404`), and so is a caller with a credential naming a venue other than the one they act at, so the answer says nothing about which other venues exist.

| Read routing | replica |
| Guest callable | True |
| Reads | `catalogue.event` |
| Writes | - |
| Called by | BO-015, BO-019, BO-1049, BO-694, CMS-101, GST-005 |

### listPerformances

**`GET /events/{eventId}/performances`**: List performances of an event

**A guest calls this without a permission and sees published data only** (Chinmay, 3 October 2026, Pattern 4; CHG-GCF-001; `x-ticvai-guest-callable`). What that means (ADAM CR-305 G1, option A, Chinmay, 29 October 2026; CHG-CR305-001): a guest or a visitor sees a performance whose `status` is `onSale` or `soldOut`, and only when its event is one `listEvents` shows a guest (`onSale` or `live`, and `isActive`); for any other event the path's event answers `404 not-found`. `PRODUCT_VIEW` is what a staff caller must hold, and staff see every `status` (ADR-0025).
**Order.** Soonest first, by `startsAt` ascending, then by `id`.
**Statuses.** For staff every `status` is returned, ended ones included (`cancelled`); a screen that shows only live rows filters on `status` itself. For a guest or a visitor only `onSale` and `soldOut`, as above.

| Part of slice | core |

| Conflict policy | serverWins |

| Guest callable | True |
| Offline note | 24 August: servable from a local cache. |
| Reads | `catalogue.performance`, `catalogue.product`, `catalogue.product_category` |
| Writes | - |
| Called by | BO-015, BO-019, BO-725, GST-003, GST-004, GST-005, GST-007, GST-049, WEB-002, WEB-004, WEB-006, WEB-007 |

### updateEvent

**`PATCH /events/{eventId}`**: Amend an event

**`parentEventId`, the group an event belongs to** (accepted 26 October 2026, Chinmay, ADAM CR-256 option A). ### updatePerformance

**`PATCH /performances/{performanceId}`**: Amend a performance

Moving a performance that has sold tickets is refused. Use cancellation, which notifies holders and refunds them — silently relocating a guest's booking is not a change, it is a surprise.

**How the refusal is decided** (ADAM CR-579, Chinmay, 3 November 2026, option A; CHG-CR579-001), in this operation's transaction under the caller's grant. (1) The performance row is read; unknown or out of scope is `404` as today. (2) **Sold tickets:** the performance has sold tickets when any `catalogue.channel_capacity` row with its `performance_id` has `sold` greater than 0, the same test event cancellation uses; it is read only when the body carries `startsAt` or `endsAt`. (3) **A timing change** is a `startsAt` or `endsAt` that differs from the stored `starts_at` or `ends_at`; a value equal to the stored one is not a change. A timing change on a performance with sold tickets is `409 performance-has-sold-tickets` and nothing is written. `status`, `language`, `format`, `minutesOnScreen` and `admissionRulesId` are accepted whether tickets are sold or not; status moves follow `states/performance.yaml` as today (`409 performance-transition-not-allowed`). (4) `admissionRulesId` is stored as sent in `admission_rules_id`. When the database refuses it on the column's foreign key to Access's admission rules, the answer is `400 validation` naming `admissionRulesId`, and nothing is written; Access's tables are not read. (5) `catalogue.channel_capacity` is read-only here: `sold` and every other column of it are never written.
